I still try to do things I usually like, like setting out my paints or thinking I’ll go to pottery class, but when I’m there I just kind of stare at everything. I don’t feel that spark or interest I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel pleasure when you're doing things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not much. It’s those thoughts like, if I didn’t wake up tomorrow, maybe that would be easier. I don’t want to hurt myself, but just having that thought has been upsetting.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most mornings lately I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like everything already feels dull before I even get dressed for work. It’s not constant every second, but it’s been there a lot.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying activities that you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Not really. I still try to do things I usually like, like setting out my paints or thinking I’ll go to pottery class, but when I’m there I just kind of stare at everything. I don’t feel that spark or interest I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel pleasure when you're doing things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not much. Like with painting, I’ll still set out my paints, or I’ll think about going to pottery class, but once I’m there I just kind of stare at the materials. I don’t feel that spark I used to get from it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed a significant change. My clothes seem to fit about the same, and I haven’t been tracking my weight closely.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been paying close attention to it, but nothing really stands out to me as a big change with my sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. It’s usually about little things at work. I’ll keep thinking about a display sign being crooked, or if a trainee looked confused, and then I start telling myself I’m a useless supervisor. It feels out of proportion, but I still get stuck on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or sluggish lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, a bit. Some days I notice I’m pacing around the living room, or picking at my cuticles without really thinking about it. Other days I feel kind of slowed down, like even walking from the break room to the sales floor feels oddly slow.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. My clothes seem to fit about the same, and I haven’t been tracking my weight closely.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been paying close attention to it, but nothing really stands out to me as a big change with my sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. It’s usually about little things at work. I’ll keep thinking about a display sign being crooked, or if a trainee looked confused, and then I start telling myself I’m a useless supervisor. It feels out of proportion, but I still get stuck on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or sluggish lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, a bit. Some days I notice I’m pacing around the living room, or picking at my cuticles without really thinking about it. I haven’t really noticed feeling especially anxious lately. I haven’t really noticed feeling especially anxious lately. Some days I do feel slowed down, like just walking from the break room to the sales floor takes more effort than it should. Not constantly, but often enough that it scared me. I’ll have thoughts like, if I just didn’t wake up tomorrow, maybe that would be easier. I don’t want to hurt myself, but having those thoughts is what pushed me to make this appointment.\n\nDoctor: How many weeks or months have you been feeling this heavy, gray feeling most mornings?\n\nPatient: About six months, I think. It’s been that low, flat feeling most mornings for long enough that even things that used to help, like my art class or morning walks, just don’t really feel restorative anymore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I still try to do things I usually like, like setting out my paints or thinking I’ll go to pottery class, but when I’m there I just kind of stare at everything. I don’t feel that spark or interest I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel pleasure when you're doing things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not much. Like with painting, I’ll still set out my paints, or I’ll think about going to pottery class, but once I’m there I just kind of stare at the materials. I don’t feel that spark I used to get from it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed a significant change. My clothes seem to fit about the same, and I haven’t been tracking my weight closely.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure.
